Refinery updates
REFINERY NEWS: Australia's Geelong processes 9.1 mil barrels during Q3
Refinery: Geelong refinery, Australia
Owner: Viva Energy
Overall capacity: 120,000 barrels/day
Australia's Geelong processed 9.1 million barrels during the third quarter of 2026, up from 8.7 million barrels in the same yearago period, Viva Energy said Oct. 22.
The intake, however, "reflected unplanned routine maintenance completed during the quarter and ongoing operational constraints" following the April 2026 fire, it said.
The alkylation unit fire, which occurred in April, impacted production at the refinery, although production has since returned to over 90% of normal capacity following the restart of the residual catalytic cracking unit and associated units in June, Viva Energy said in July.
It subsequently said in August that the Geelong refinery will maintain production above 90% of normal operating capacity until the alkylation unit is repaired or replaced.
-- Elza Turner
REFINERY NEWS: Pakistan Refinery's FY26 utilization at over 80%
Refinery: Pakistan Refinery Ltd., Korangi Industrial Area, Karachi
Owner: Pakistan Refinery Ltd.
Overall capacity: 50,000 b/d
Pakistan Refinery Ltd. operated at over 80% capacity utilization during the fiscal year ended June 30, 2026, processing about 1.72 million metric tons of crude oil to meet rising domestic demand for diesel and motor gasoline, according to the company's annual report released Oct. 21.
Stringent controls over inventory management continued to be exercised, with a focus on optimizing the crude slate and

maximizing production of higher-margin products, particularly Motor Spirit (MS or gasoline) and High-Speed Diesel (HSD), the annual report said.
The company achieved a significant operational milestone during the year by producing more than 800,000 mt of diesel for the first time in its history.
Pressure on local upliftment of high sulfur furnace oil (also known as high sulfur fuel oil) continued to increase during the year.
The imposition of Petroleum and Climate Support Levies on HSFO further impacted its economic viability in the domestic market, resulting in negligible local demand and requiring the company to export a substantial portion of its HSFO production.
During the year, the refinery exported 386,838 mt of HSFO, representing 98.3% of total HSFO sales, compared with 311,570 mt or a 71.2% share in the previous year, the report said.
Refinery upgrade plans Pakistan Refinery is pursuing a modernization and expansion
plan aimed at improving product quality, increasing processing capacity and reducing furnace oil production.
The proposed upgrade includes producing Euro V-compliant high-speed diesel and motor spirit, expanding crude oil processing capacity from 50,000 barrels/day to 100,000 b/d, and transforming the facility from a hydroskimming refinery into a deep-conversion refinery.
The transition to deep conversion is intended to significantly reduce HSFO output and enable the refinery to produce a greater proportion of higher-value petroleum products
-- Haris Zamir
REFINERY NEWS: Germany's Holborn signs contract for hydrogen delivery
Refinery: Holborn, Harburg, Hamburg, Germany
Owner: Oilinvest Group
Overall capacity: 105,000 barrels/day
Germany's Holborn refinery has signed a contract with Hamburger Energienetze for the planning and connection to the hydrogen network, it said in a statement Oct. 22.
From 2028, the hydrogen will be supplied to the GDP complex (green diesel production), which is currently under construction at the Harburg refinery site.
The refinery has previously said the complex construction started in September 2024 and the plant targets the first biofuel production from 2027.
The GDP complex will produce around 220,000 metric tons of HVO and sustainable aviation fuel per year based on used cooking oils and other biogenic by-products, and requires the use of hydrogen, it also said Oct. 22.
-- Elza Turner

REFINERY NEWS: India's Manali Q2 processing down 13.9% year over year
Refinery: Manali, Chennai, South India
Owner: Chennai Petroleum Corp. Ltd.
Overall capacity: 210,000 b/d
India's state-owned Chennai Petroleum Corp. Ltd. recorded a 13.9% year-over-year fall in crude processing for the second quarter, which runs from July to September, to 2.59 million metric tons, company officials said Oct. 22.
They attributed the lower processing to a planned maintenance of one of the crude distillation units during the second quarter.
Manali refinery's fiscal year runs from April to March, according to the company.
In Q2, crude throughput was 5.7% higher than the 2.45 million mt processed in the April-June period, or Q1, they said.
For the April-September period, or H1, the refiner processed 5.44 million mt, down 9.2% from a year ago, company officials said, mainly due to maintenance work carried out during the second quarter.
In Q2, Manali recorded a $20.4/b gross refining margin compared with $9/b a year ago, reflecting a higher return from cracks, they added.
In H1, Manali's GRM stood at $14.3/b compared with $6.2/b a year ago, they said.
In Q2, Manali's capacity utilization stood at 98%, while its runs were at 103% for the six-month review period, the officials said.
In 2025-26 (April-March), Manali recorded crude throughput of 11.7 million mt, up 11.5% from a year ago, according to the oil ministry update.
Manali mainly produces LPG, motor spirit, superior kerosene, aviation turbine fuel, high-speed diesel, naphtha, bitumen, lube base stocks, paraffin wax, fuel oil, hexane and petroleum feedstocks, the officials said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta
REFINERY NEWS: Valero targets near-record Q4 runs on wide crude discounts, tight markets
Refinery: 14 refineries in the US, UK and Canada
Owner: Valero
Overall capacity: 3 million b/d
Units affected: N/A
Duration: Q4
Valero Energy is targeting near-record refinery utilization in the fourth quarter of 2026, with US Gulf Coast throughput guided as high as 1.905 million barrels/day as the company takes advantage of the wide crude differentials and structurally tight transportation fuel markets heading into year-end, company executives said on Oct. 22.
Valero's Q4 guidance, provided on the company's third-quarter

results call, signals a light maintenance schedule across all four operating regions, with management describing the quarter as an opportunity to run assets hard while margins remain elevated.
"We are -- it is a light maintenance quarter for us -- and so we'll run the assets hard with margins where they are," Gary Simmons, Valero's COO, said on the third-quarter results call.
Simmons said Valero does not really defer maintenance and will continue with its turnaround schedule.
"What you do hear from consultants is that there was about 1.5 million b/d of refinery turnarounds globally that was deferred, which means turnaround activity is currently a little lighter than normal, and you would expect a heavier turnaround season next year," he added.
Lower Q4 turnaround activity is reflected in Valero's lower refining cash operating expenses, which are expected to fall to about $4.85/barrel in Q4, down from $5.03/b in Q3. In Q4, USGC throughput is guided at 1.855 million-1.9 million b/d, alongside guidance of Mid-Continent throughput at 440,000-460,000 b/d, North Atlantic throughput at 480,000-500,000 b/d, and West Coast throughput at 100,000-110,000 b/d.
Simmons identified several tailwinds that should support Q4 capture rates: butane blending uplift into winter-grade gasoline specifications, Arctic diesel premiums in the North Atlantic system, continued discounts on heavy sour feedstocks, and a recovery in jet fuel crack spreads.
However, Valero's Q4 throughput guidance does not include any contribution from the diesel hydrotreater unit at Valero's Port Arthur, Texas, refinery, which was damaged in a fire earlier in 2026.
Simmons confirmed mechanical completion of the DHT is not expected until the end of the quarter, adding the refinery has continued to operate at normal throughput rates throughout the repair period.
Crude discounts shift, VGO tight The Q4 throughput targets are underpinned by a shifting
crude procurement landscape that Valero's commercial team said is moving in the company's favor.
Randy Hawkins, head of Valero's crude and feedstocks supply, said Canadian crude discounts in the USGC had widened to as much as $27-$30/b under ICE Brent futures in early Q4, making Canadian barrels significantly more competitive than Latin American alternatives after freight costs are factored in.
Valero set a new quarterly processing record for Venezuelan barrels in Q3 due to favorable economics, but Hawkins said the system's flexibility to switch between grades represented a key competitive advantage as economics shifted.
The broader crude differential environment has been driven by a surge in global freight rates.
Hawkins said the cost to move a Very Large Crude Carrier from the USGC to Asia had risen to approximately $30-$35/b, compared with a 2025 average of around $5/b, forcing sellers of crude in distant markets to widen discounts to account for the freight burden.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 22, 2026

"The Gulf Coast is also freight advantaged for heavy barrels out of Latin America compared with the Asian alternative," Hawkins said, adding that discounts for foreign barrels were having to compensate for the large freight increase.
On the feedstock side, CEO Lane Riggs identified two priority areas for future growth capital: reducing dependence on externally sourced vacuum gasoil and expanding heavy sour crude processing capacity.
Riggs described the US refining system as structurally short VGO -- a key intermediate feedstock for fluid catalytic crackers -- a condition intensified by the loss of supply flows previously sourced from Ukraine and broader European markets.
Riggs identified debottlenecking internal VGO production and expanding heavy sour crude intake, particularly from Canada and Venezuela, as the highest-priority investment buckets for future capital allocation.
However, Q3's most significant milestone was the onschedule, on-budget finish of a $230 million fluid catalytic cracker unit optimization project at the St. Charles refinery in Louisiana.
The project was designed to more fully utilize existing alkylation capacity at the site, boosting output of alkylate and finished gasoline, with the greatest benefits to be seen in Q2 and Q3 of each year when gasoline crack spreads are typically strongest, according to Valero.
Ethanol capacity creep aided by PTC In renewable diesel segment, Valero's joint-venture Diamond
Green Diesel reported sales volumes averaged 3.4 million gallons/ day in Q3.
Valero's partner, Darling Ingredients, expects Q4 volumes to average 3.25 million gal/day due to planned work underway at the DGD facility located at Port Arthur, Texas, according to an earlier statement.
DGD can produce 1.2 billion gal/year of renewable diesel and sustainable aviation fuel on the USGC.
In its solely owned ethanol segment, Valero achieved a quarterly throughput record of 4.8 million gal/day at its 12 US ethanol plant located in the US Midwest in Q3, with a combined production capacity of 1.7 billion gal/year, or about 4.66 million gal/day.
The company expects Q4 ethanol production to rise to 4.9 million gal/day, adding it has been "pursuing efficiency and capacity expansion projects" at its ethanol plants encouraged by the new Production Tax Credit.
"It's encouraging really all ethanol producers to take a look at higher volumes," Eric Fisher said of the PTC incentive and 45Z credits. "And so we are looking at mostly capacity creep. ....small capacity creep."
"We're continuing to pursue our carbon sequestration projects at a couple of our sites," he added. "We are hoping those are hitting somewhere between late '27 to mid-'28. All of that will grow both ethanol volume as well as lower ethanol [Carbon Intensity], which makes it more attractive for exports. So we continue to see pretty strong export demand for ethanol and our capacity is all pointed at growing exports."

Valero operates 14 petroleum refineries located in the US, Canada, and the UK with a combined throughput capacity of approximately 3 million b/d. Valero is a joint venture member in Diamond Green Diesel Holdings LLC, which produces lowcarbon fuels, including RD and SAF, with a production capacity of approximately 1.2 billion gal/year in the USGC. See the annual report on Form 10-K for more information on SAF.
Valero also owns 12 ethanol plants located in the US MidContinent region with a combined production capacity of about 1.7 billion gal/year.
-- Janet Mcgurty

Owner: Hindustan Petroleum Corp. Ltd.
Overall capacity: 24.5 million mt
India's No. 3 state-run refiner, Hindustan Petroleum Corp. Ltd., processed 6.2 million metric tons in the July-September period, or the second quarter, the company said Oct. 22, reflecting 100% capacity utilization for the combined runs at its two refineries at Vizag and Mumbai during the second quarter.

In Q2, Mumbai processed 2.6 million mt, reflecting 109% runs, it added.
In Q2, HPCL's combined distillate yield stood at 75.3%, the company said in a regulatory filing.

In Q2, HPCL's domestic sales grew by 4.9% year over year, the company said.
HPCL's sales of motor fuels stood at 7.8 million mt in Q2, up 10% year over year, it said.

Subscriber Notes

Platts to launch forward derivative assessments for STS Lome gasoline, diesel and jet fuel
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has noted increasing demand for transparency on West African refined products, both on a spot market basis and for forward delivery. In response, Platts will start publishing daily forward swap assessments for the existing Gasoline STS Lome, Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome, and Jet Fuel STS Lome physical price assessments, effective Nov. 30, 2026. These will be published as Month 1, Month 2, and Month 3 assessments.
In order to assess the derivatives on an editorial basis, Platts will consult a range of market information, including information heard in the physical market, relevant forward exchange-traded data, as well as related markets.
The published assessments will correspond with delivery dates three months forward, starting with the front month [M1], followed by M2 and M3.

Assessment Gasoline STS Lome Financial Mo01 Gasoline STS Lome Financial Mo02 Gasoline STS Lome Financial Mo03 Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome Financial Mo01 Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome Financial Mo02 Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome Financial Mo03 Jet Fuel STS Lome Financial Mo01 Jet Fuel STS Lome Financial Mo02 Jet Fuel STS Lome Financial Mo03

Symbol STSLA00 STSLB00 STSLC00 STSLD00 STSLE00 STSLF00 STSLG00 STSLH00 STSLI00

The assessments will reflect value at 16:30 London time. Heard bids/offers and trades will be reported in real time throughout the day on Platts PGA005, along with other relevant market information, and the information published will be used to inform the physical assessment.
The new assessments can be found in European Marketscan and on Platts Global Alert and Platts Refined Products Alert fixed page 1122.
Platts continues to monitor evolving West Africa oil product flows and seeks industry feedback on its existing West African refined oil products assessment methodology.

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

To be announced

Platts will assess the differential in $/metric ton, reflecting the difference between the relevant STS Lome gasoline assessment and the physical Platts Eurobob FOB AR assessment.
Platts will publish the new assessments in $/mt, timestamped at 4.30 pm London time, as well as a monthly average.
The new gasoline assessments will be found in European Marketscan and on Platts Global Alert and Platts Refined Products Alert fixed page 1122.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

22

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 22, 2026

Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).
The affected assessments appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Oilgram Price Report, LNG Daily, Platts OPR World Crude Table, Platts Global Alert/Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 1250, 1805, 2202, 2203, 2211, 2216, 2217, 2244, 2245, 2264, 2265, 2280, 2281, 2811 and 2812, LNG Alert pages 0050 and 0051, Platts Natural Gas Alert pages 1024 and 1069, and under the price database codes below:

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication of
Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from

November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Diff to Dubai AOSPA00 AOSPB00 AOSPC00 AOSPD00

Diff to Murban OSP
AOSPE00 AOSPF00 AOSPG00

ADNOC's OSPs are published in the Platts price database and on Platts Global Alert page 1051.

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Assessment FOB Middle East Bitumen VG30 FOB Middle East Bitumen VG40

Quantity 2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 2,500-10,000 mt, bulk

Laycan 10-30 days forward 10-30 days forward

UoM $/mt $/mt

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.
This follows a proposal published Sept. 14 and available here. Please send any questions, comments or feedback to asia_products@spglobal.com, with a copy to pricegroup@spglobal.com. For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make those not marked as confidential available upon request.
Platts proposes to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of the month, from Dec. 1, 2026.
The proposed change, which follows Platts review of historical data sets, would align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of its ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward value of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266,

Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 22, 2026

The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by their corresponding weightings.
For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to 20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol
AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name
ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name
Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket

Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here.
Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi waiver in multiple Midwest states and a division in RVP.
Platts is also proposing to launch a separate West Shore/ Badger assessment for Chicago ULSD; this would not replace the

existing generic assessment, which would remain unchanged. The name of the new assessment would be ULSD Chicago West Shore and Badger Pipeline.
The indexes would appear on PGA pages 350, 351, 154, 24, 482, 410 and 411, as well as in US Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, and on Platts Connect.
Please send all feedback and comments by Jan. 23 to americas_products@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.
Platts invites feedback on US gasoline RVP calendar for 2026 As part of its commitment to open and transparent pricing
and product specifications, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, would like to invite feedback regarding publishing dates and/or cycles for 2026 US gasoline RVP shifts.
The 2026 calendar largely follows 2025 norms, with no notable deviations from prior years. The calendar will be updated throughout the year as more information becomes available.
The shift dates are based on relevant pipeline schedules, historical information, and feedback from market participants.
US Gulf Coast shifts are based on the Colonial Pipeline schedule. US Atlantic Coast shifts are based on Buckeye Pipeline and Colonial Pipeline calendars.
On the West Coast, Los Angeles, San Francisco and Arizona RVP changes are based on Kinder Morgan's SFPP schedule, while Portland and Seattle shifts are based on Olympic Pipeline's calendar.
In the Midwest, Group 3 shifts are based on the ONEOK Pipeline schedule, while Chicago shifts are based on Explorer Pipeline and Buckeye Complex schedules.
The US Environmental Protection Agency removed a waiver in eight Midwestern states that allowed for an additional 1 psi during the summer. Platts will adjust the RVP Calendar for Chicago CBOB in accordance with the implementation of the waiver. More information can be found here.
For questions or comments, please contact americas_products@spglobal.com with a cc to pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
